## Heldon

Heldon was a farming village in outlying Athalantar until the, later the site of an armed camp.

Description
The city had a smithy, a mill, and at least two towers of different heights.

Geography
To the south of Heldon was a curve of the Delimbiyr River. At a higher elevation than the settlement proper, there was a meadow. A high ravine separated it from Heldon, and there was a cave on the end of that ravine. West of Heldon were the Horn Hills.

Trade
Sheep were raised around Heldon.

History
In, Prince Elthryn Aumar married Amrythale Goldsheaf of Heldon. Of note, their son Elminster Aumar was born two years later.

The village was razed by Undarl, riding the red dragon Glathkarlarrogh, on behalf of the Magelords of Athalantar, in the ; he was there to slay Elthryn and his kin, and slew everyone in sight for good measure. There was only one survivor: Elthryn's son, Elminster, whom Undarl left for dead early on.

After its destruction, a palisade wall was built around a new armed camp at some point on or prior to .

Notable Locations
There was a shrine to Chauntea south of Heldon.

Of note, there was a meadow atop the village, and a cave hidden somewhere under that meadow, known to almost none outside Elminster.

Inhabitants
Elthryn Aumar
Amrythale Goldsheaf
Tesla
Elminster Aumar
Tassabra

## Hephtos

Hephtos was a dwarven street vendor, cleric of Vergadain, and author who lived in the city of Ravens Bluff in the 14 century DR. However, even though worship of the Morndinsamman was legal and tolerated in the city, Hephtos chose to masquerade as a Tymoran cleric, one of the gods of Ravens Bluff's Civic Religion. He hoped that in doing, so he would improve his chances of ingratiating himself to both the Clerical Circle and the city's human populace, thereby lowering their guards and making his endeavors to coerce people out of their valuables that much easier.

Description
Hephthos was a man of portly build who stood  tall. He had brown eyes and a deep voice. And in his old age, the man's once red hair was balding and showing gray streaks.

Abilities
In addition to clerical spells, Hephthos was notably adept at cooking, building fires, handling animals, singing, and appraising the value of gems and jewelry. His arsenal of spells included cure light wounds, command, detect magic, enthrall, sanctuary, speak with animals, and withdraw.

Possessions
Under his plain monastic robes Hepthos wore a suit of splint mail, black boots, and a belt pouch where he kept his spell components and potions of healing. Upon his belt he also kept his trusty morningstar within ready reach. While doing his street vendor work, he often wore a grease-stained apron atop the robes. He also proudly displayed holy symbols of Tymora & Vergadain around his neck, though the latter, a gold coin hung from a leather thong, was less conspicuous compared to the Tymoran silver disk.

The dwarf also owned a number of magic items that he utilized in his schemes. These included a ring of human influence, a ring of protection +1, a stone of good luck, and horseshoes of speed. He had these horseshoes mounted upon his pet Sal, a trained one-eyed mule.

The food cart that Sal hauled around for the dwarf was brightly painted and kept stocked with foodstuffs, supplies, and utensils. It was also outfitted with a stove and oven. Additionally, the cart held the dwarf's other personal effects, such as a bedroll and the occasional line of laundry, strung from the chimney to the wooden front seat.

Activities
Often operating under the alias of Friar Cookpot, Hephthos started each day in Ravens Bluff by moving his food cart to either across the street from the Open Air Farmers Market, outside the city in Tent Town, or in the neighborhood of Seaglimpse just outside the Gates of the Watchful Raven. Once settled in a prominent spot, he would put on something aromatic to cook and break into song, all part of an effort to attract customers. However, his singing and boisterous style of cooking also served to masquerade what were in reality verbal and somatic components of his spells.

His skills in cooking were overall quite extraordinary, though his repertoire was largely limited to baked goods and dishes that involved beans. Hephthos was so enamored with the use of beans in cooking that he even authored two cookbooks, 1001 Ways to Cook Beans and Cobblers and Pies: Baked Beans Can Be Fun. By far his most favorite recipe, one that garnered him some notoriety, was L'Orange Beans Flambe with Slag Sauce.

Whilst he sold copies of his books from the cart, Hephthos passed out servings of whatever he cooked to crowds free of charge. He would then bring out a correction plate for donations, using that moment of close proximity to activate his ring of human influence and thus coerce people into giving him their valuables. If by any chance he was caught charming someone, the dwarf would cast sanctuary and work to disappear into the throng of people.

## Helena Falagon

Helena Falagon was an Evenor villager in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Helena had tanned skin and long brown hair, wearing a dark red dress.

History
Early in the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Helena took care of the poor servant Maisby, who had just escaped from the nearby Midler farmstead. The priestess of Lolth Vicala Szind had taken over the farm and captured the Midler family, interrogating them for information about the Thread of the Weave. Helena had Adamor Thellik send two Purple Dragons to investigate, only for them to disappear, and next wrangled some adventurers to assist. The ensuing battle led to the destruction of the drow forces and the rescue of the Midler family.

## Hearth Warmth

The Hearth Warmth was an inn in the village of Anechu in T'u Lung. According to Tan Hikao Nong, it served the finest food within the country.

## Hetta Matrell

Hetta Debrinne Matrell was the founding matriarch of House Matrell, a noble mercantile family of Arrabar.

Personality
She was a very resourceful, resolute, and sweet old woman. She had a voice that was soft, though commanded attention. She was also a, self-professed, stubborn individual.

She had a shrewd business sense and was skilled at reading people. She possessed an uncanny ability for figuring out exactly what someone was planning or thinking about, and therefore what their likely reaction would be in any given situation.

Possessions
Hetta wore on the fourth finger of her right hand a silver, filigreed ring inset with a ruby. This ring had an enchantment that made it so that, in the case of her death, her spirit would be drawn into the ring instead of letting it transition into the afterlife. She had this made not for the purpose of extending her life, but to ensure that at least one member of House Matrell was well equipped to take over the mercantile family's business operations in the event of her suffering an untimely demise.

Whilst her spirit was inside the ring, Hetta's mind would remain conscious and be capable of telepathically communicating with whosoever held the ring, as well as telepathically read their minds. Her spirit could eventually be returned to its body, though there was a limit to how much time could pass before this became nonviable. Additionally, it depended upon what condition her body was in.

The spirit bound within the ring could also possess the body of any another sentient creature that handled it, switching the placement of their minds and souls. And it could pass on some of its energy to anyone wearing the ring, healing injuries.

Relationships
Over the course of Hetta's marriage with Obiron Matrell I she had four children. In order of birth, these were Obril, Hesta, Dregaul, and Xaphira. Of the four Xaphira was the scamp of the family, the ambitious and tomboyish spitfire driving her family mad, most of all her father. However, she was also the child that Hetta felt the closest to, for she saw a lot of herself in the girl.

When Obril sired Emriana with Ladara Lazelle, Hetta felt much the same way about the girl, seeing in her the same ambitious spirit. She always encouraged her to follow her own path and not conform to the traditional Arrabaran society expectations towards their gender. She also had a positive relationship with their son Vambran, though when he was a youth she would spank him and his friends whenever they crushed her flowers in the process of using her garden wall as a shortcut.

Ladara Lazelle was a very dutiful daughter-in-law to the old woman, meekly following her around everywhere, spending much of her time providing Hetta assistance.

Hetta had connections with a number of Arrabar's greater houses, her great business acumen being respected in many Arrabarran social circles. Among them she counted Ariskrit Darowdryn, fellow noble matriarch of House Darowdryn, as one of her oldest friends and confidants. The women were often seen socializing during events of the Arrabarran nobility and often visited each other at their families' estates.

Activities
Whilst she was in charge of the estate, Hetta never allowed the soldiers that House Matrell employed as guards to smoke on duty, feeling that it would be a distraction to their duties.

She kept long hours, even well into her eighties.

In her old age Hetta continued to maintain her position as head of the Matrell household, but allowed her family to believe she left the entirety of House Matrell's day-to-day operations under the handling of her son Dregaul. In actuality, being distrustful of the lad, feeling he had a poor business sense and lack of understanding of what she and Obiron were trying to build, she entrusted only one tenth of their holdings to him. The rest of the family's assets were built up in secrecy over the course of eleven years through shrewd investments made with her daughter Xaphira, who at the time was separated from the rest of the Matrells. She kept this charade up in hopes that Dregaul would one day come to a true understanding of business.

History
Hetta was born on Alturiak 9, in the .

She had her first child with Obiron, Hesta Matrell, on Alturiak 7, 1315 DR. Her next child, Obril, would be born on Ches 25, 1318 DR. Dregaul would follow on Eleint 2, 1320 DR. And her last child, Xaphira, would be born on Ches 17, 1336 DR.

As Hetta and Obiron raised their children, the Matrells were largely ostracized by the other noble families of Arrabar, on account of being a young merchant house with no social weight behind their name. A factor that was further aggravated by her husband, Obiron Matrell, being a denounced bastard-born of House Mestel. However, through her wit and knack for reading people, the couple managed to be quite successful businessmen. Whilst Obiron acted as the spokesperson in their business dealings, Hetta was always his trusted advisor on what was the right course of action to take. Under their joint ministrations the family business grew quite rapidly, achieving a strong and diversified portfolio by the time that their grandson Vambran Matrell was born in 1350 DR. And thanks to her shrewd and fair dealings with others, Hetta managed to get the Matrells invited to a number of coming-of-age birthday parties over the years.

In the, Hetta's husband tragically passed away. Shortly after the death of her husband, Hetta had her enchanted ring made, feeling uncertain that any of her children understood all of the nuances of business enough to take over House Matrell from her should she pass. Over the next few years the family's business operations would be handled jointly by Hetta, Dregaul, and Evester Matrell. As the years passed and Dregaul showed no signs of true business acumen, Hetta felt tempted to take full control over House Matrell away from him. Yet she kept staying her hand, hoping that one day he would either see the light.

In the month of Flamerule, in the, the members of House Matrell were attending the Night of Ghosts festival, hosted by Eles Wianar at the Generon. That night Hetta's grandson Vambran, along with two of his young friends and fellow noble scions, Adyan Mercatio and Horial Rohden, were playing around in the palace garden with a bejeweled crossbow that had been gifted to him by his uncle Kovrim Matrell. Whilst shooting plantains off a tree, Vambran unleashed a stray bolt and before they knew it a bolt was lodged in the chest of Rodolpho Wianar, a cousin of the Lord of Arrabar. However, young Vambran had not actually slayed Rodolpho that night. An assassin by the name of Junce Roundface had stolen a bolt from his quiver that evening and used it, at the behest of Grand Trabbar Lavant and Lord Eles, to fake the nobleman's death and frame the young Vambran for murder, all part of a machiavellian scheme of conquest that would take the next twelve years to unfold.

Xaphira and Dregaul knew that this accidental murder meant death for Vambran and ruin for their House. Desperate to see her nephew unharmed, Xaphira took the blame. She masqueraded as a masked assassin and fled Arrabar, knowing she may never be able to return. She was presumed dead by all but Hetta, who stayed in contact with Xaphira over the next eleven years. Together the two made several shrewd investments that ended up bolstering House Matrell's holdings.

As the years went on, Hetta's hope of Dregaul seeing the light soured. as she recognized Dregaul's business sense had fallen down an immoral slope. One where any business deal was a good decision, regardless of questionable it may be either financially or ethically. However, she had no idea that Dregaul had been teaching his reprehensible business practices to her grandson, Evester Matrell. Her reasoning for not usurping control from him changed to a new twofold reasoning. On one hand she anticipated he would get so in over his head with a scheme that it would show everyone else in the Matrell family that, not only was he unfit to run the household, but that Hetta was still as sharp as ever. On the other, she needed to let it go on so she could determine who in the family she could trust, who would recognize their House was unstable and needed fixing.
Sapphire Crescent
On Tarsakh 10, Vambran Mattrell returned home to Arrabar to celebrate his sister Emriana's sixteenth birthday. On their way home, the two stumbled upon the scene of two commoners murdered at the hands of the Municipal Guard of Arrabar, in an alley near their estate. He would later identify these commoners as Hiyote and Jithelle Skolotti. When they finally arrived back at the Matrell estate late into the evening with Emriana he got into a heated argument with his uncle Dregaul at the dining room table, receiving scorn for endangering his sister and dismissal of his goodly intentions. Vambran even subtly suggested that he was tempted to leave the Matrell family, shocking even Hetta.

Eventually the conflict was broken up by Hetta, whose commanding tone drove everyone gathered in the room to silence and redirected their attention. Exhaustion and sorrow evident on her face, Hetta told them she was too old to watch the two of them squabbling so. She expressed her dissapointment towards Dregaul's conspicuous and lengthy disdain towards Vambran, telling him it was high time he let it go, and told Vambran to cease any talk of leaving House Matrell.

Hetta then reached out a hand to her daughter-in-law, Ladara Lazelle, who quickly provided the elder matriarch support as she rose to her feet. Once on her feet Hetta further her scolding of the two men, pointing out that it was late and everyone was tired. She ordered the two of them to put aside their feud, wanting to hear no more of it that night, and postpone it to tomorrow after breakfast.

Since well before Tarsakh 11, Hetta had been planning to have tea with House Pharaboldi at their estate, alongside Emriana and Ladara Lazelle. She instructed the handmaiden Jaleene to ensure that Emriana was up and ready to leave the next day by eight bells and to inform the girl that she wanted to talk with her over morningfeast before they all departed. When Emriana went downstairs after her bath she found Hetta sitting at the head of their large dining table. Hetta dabbed herself with a napkin, smiled at her granddaughter, and then gestured her to sit by patting the seat next to her. As she got close Hetta kissed her on the forehead and one of the serving women emerged with a platter of scrambled eggs, mixed with cheese and a sauce made of lemon and wine. The woman scooped a spoonful of eggs onto Emriana's plate, whilst other servers brought out a further array of food for the morningfeast.

As her granddaughter began to eat Hetta cleared her throat and stated her desire to discuss what had occurred the night prior. She then swiftly told the girl to not put on a brave face for the conversation. As Emriana bemoaned wanting freedom and independence from Dregaul, Hetta agreed with her that she was old enough to make her own decisions and be respected as an adult, but told the girl that she had to show her uncle that she was capable of making good decisions. Hetta then asked Emriana if she remembered Xaphira and expressed how the girl reminded her of her aunt and thus expected great things of her. This provoked Emriana into asking about what happened to her aunt, to which Hetta answered vaguely and warned her granddaughter that she was to speak of it to no one else, for if the story was to come to light it would still pose issues for House Matrell.

Before they finished their morningfeast Hetta revealed to Emriana that she had informed Dregaul that he was not to relieve the handmaidens from her duties, though was permitted to make threats of doing so if it would make him feel any better. With morningfeast complete, the three Matrell women headed out by coach to the Pharaboldi estate, accompanied by an entourage. As they made their way through Arrabar's Merchant District, Hetta engaged in small talk with Ladara and their attendants, discussing the latest gossip.

When they finally arrived at the estate, attendants approached the cart and helped each of the women climb out, starting with Ladara, followed by Hetta and then Emriana, whose chose to go last so as to ensure that neither of the older women lost their footing and fell. From the tiled porch above, the three women were greeted by the Pharaboldi matriarch, Anista, who ushered them inside from the subtropical heat. Inside the open-air mansion they were led to a large, garden-like central chamber, where Anista announce they would have a proper midday meal in the dining room after they visited for a bit over tea. A serving woman came out shortly to present them some aromatic Amnian tea and thin wafers, made of sweetened bread topped with whipped honey. Her granddaughter would not have long to spend with them, for the hostess encouraged her to go spend time with her son Derrick Pharaboldi, who had previously been trying to court her. With a knowing glare from her mother Ladara, Emriana felt pressured into agreeing with Anista.

Meanwhile, Vambran was meeting with his uncle Kovrim and the two headed to Dark She Looks Upon Me, a popular Arrabarran eatery. There, the young lieutenant was ambushed by a band of mysterious assassins who were hellbent on stopping Vambran's investigation. With help from a mysterious red cloaked figure, who he would later learn to be aunt Xaphira, Vambran barely survived the assault. Kovrim arrived just in time to heal his nephew's wounds using his clerical magic. He then brought Vambran back to the Matrell estate in a carriage, where he was watched over by Hetta and half the family until he awoke, with Hetta and Emriana sitting on either side of his head.  When he finally awoke, recovered from his injuries, he requested a moment alone with uncle Kovrim. With a simple nod Hetta obliged the boy, turning to leave and Ladara quickly sprang to her feet to help the matriarch out.

On Tarsakh 13, the day of Emriana's sixteenth birthday, Hetta was the first to greet her and Vambran at the base of the garden stairs, right Ladara right by her side ready to provide her aid. She received a warm smile from her granddaughter and knelt down to kiss her on the cheek. Full of pride, she remarked, "My little Elm is all grown up. Dazzle them, dear." The rest of her close family proceeded with their own greetings of the girl, until finally Hetta told them to let her pass, for she had guests to greet.Chapter 1 of The Sapphire Crescent opens with the current date of 10 Tarsakh. In chapter 2, page 28, Emriana states that her birthday "is in three days", which would indicate that her birthday falls on Tarsakh 13. Due to the many connections that Hetta had made over the years and the fairness in which she conducted business, a great deal of people turned up to the celebration to honor Emriana on her grandmother's behalf, including members of several other merchant Houses, such as Ariskrit Darowdryn and her full plated nephew Tharlgarl "Steelfists" Darowdryn, several members of House Elphaendim, and the loathsome Denrick Pharaboldi.

Later that evening, Vambran and Embriana were interrogating Denrick over the murder of Jithelle Skolotti, a  servant girl he had previously been sleeping with and gotten pregnant. In the midst of this they heard a loud and piercing scream emanate from the other side of the Matrell estate. It came from their mother Ladara, reacting to the sight of Hetta being struck in the calf by a crossbow bolt and crumbling to the ground. Unseen by all present, the attack had been conducted by Grozier Talricci, who felt that they had few other options to dissuade Vambran's ever persistent investigation into Jithelle's murder and by proxy his conspiracy with Dregaul.

The two jolted as fast as they could to the source of the anguished cry. With Vambran the quicker of the two, he got to the injured body of Hetta first, kneeling down in the grass amidst a gathered crowd and a sobbing Ladara, who in a panick kept urging him to do something. Hetta's breath was rapid and shallow, whilst her open eyes were glazed over and staring off into the distance.

When Emriana arrived on the scene someone else was shouting at people to get back. She looked on helplessly as her brother concentrated on first snapping bolt's head before extracting the rest, wanting to avoid any further injury. As Hetta lurch in pain and issued feeble cries, her granddaughter moved in close and knelt beside her. Once the extraction was completed, Vambran placed his hands on either side of the wound and began to chant a prayer to Waukeen. Though lacking his divine magic, Embriana joined her brother, praying to Waukeen that Hetta would live.

Eventually Hetta jolted up with a gasp, proclaiming "So cold!" before looking from side to side, the look in her eyes back to being clear and focused. She then proceeded to reach for her leg, as if she had only just then realized she was shot. A concerned Vambran told the matriarch to ease herself, telling her that she would be fine, all the while gently forcing her to lay back down in the grass. With a sob of relief Ladara hugged her son, then put a trembling hand to her mouth as she patted the old woman on the cheek. Vambran wasted no time in questioning the surrounding guests in what they had witnessed, but none of them knew where the shot had originated from. Then a house guard stumbled out from the underbrush, presenting the culprit's crossbow, which had attached a note that read, "Next time, it will be her heart that I pierce."

Snatching the crossbow out of the terrified guardsman's hands, Vambran sprinted down in the direction where the assassin had likely ran. Several men chased after Vambran, wanting to aid him in catching the criminal, whilst more stood guard to protect against any further attempts on Hetta's life. Emriana was beside herself, feeling that the note indicated the attack was a warning directed at her and Vambran. All the while Ladara never left Hetta's side. She kept insisting to her daughter that she go fetch things for her, even though several servants were standing around. For a time Emriana put off her mother's requests, sending their servants to go fetch the desired items, until Ladara told her in no uncertain terms to get Hetta's house robes.

However, away from their prying eyes, Dregaul verbally and physically assaulted the young woman. He threatened to have her locked up and tortured in their dungeon lest she put on a smile and behave in front of "his guests". Then once they were out amongst the crowd of guests that remained, Dregaul announced that Embriana was to wed Denrick, creating an alliance between Houses Matrell and Pharaboldi, and feeling sick to her stomach at the revelation the young woman ran off.

Later in the evening Dregaul was gathered in a study with his fellow conspirators, Grozier Talricci, the Talricci House mage Bartimus, and Anista Pharaboldi to discuss the financial concerns of the army they were going to muster with their pooled resources. They hoped to buy in early enough that they could get a full summer campaign out of them, waging an invasion of another city for profit. Dregaul was arguing with them about profit-splits and plainly quite agitated over his co-conspirator having shot Hetta. But just as their talks were underway Hetta slammed open the door and stormed into the study with a furious gaze, accompanied by Ladara and two house guards. The conspirators all jumped in alarm, but most of all Dregaul. Addressing her son sternly, the Matrell matriarch proclaimed, "this travesty of a business decision you've made has seen its last days."

Turning in his chair, Dregaul demanded to know what she was doing there and insisted that she needed rest. But the matriarch quickly rebuffed him, pointing out it was foolish to think he could deceive her and stating, "I have no intention of resting while my son is bringing my House down around all of our ears." She chastised his decision to mortgage their finances to their limits for their small profit-driven war, even as Dregaul desperately tried to argue that this venture could make House Matrell stand alongside Arrabar's big mercantile Houses. Putting her foot down as the matriarch, saying she had final say on all business ventures, she turned to Anista and Grozier and informed them that their deal with the Matrells was off.

Grozier tried to shut the old woman down, alerting her that they had already hired armies and issued marching orders, but Hetta simply drew up to her full height and sternly responded that he would either have to find a way to stop these "things" that were in motions or find other business partners. Seething with rage at her interference, Dregaul arose from his chair and shouted at her to stop. Calling her past her prime and lacking the clarity of thought to see how important this venture was to House Matrell's future in Arrabar, he expressed that it was high time she "officially stepped down and completely handed the reins over". This illicited a hearty laughed from the matriarch, who had to put a hand out to Ladara for support. Further enraged, Dregaul denigrated the work she and Obiron had done, leaving the old woman hurt and shacking her head in disagreement.

Finally, Hetta expressed regret over having failed to properly teach him that their family was never about clawing their way up the social hierarchy of Arrabar, then declared Dregaul stripped over his responibilities regarding Matrell's business interests, intending to take full control of them herself. Hetta then turned to the pair of guards in the doorway, told them to lock Dregaul up in the estate's basement if he refused to come quietly, and told his fellow conspirators to depart as well. However, the loyalty of the house guards having turned to Dregaul, they blocked Hetta from exiting the study.

Whilst Hetta, Ladara, many other family members were locked in a cell in the basement, Vambran, Xaphira, and Kovrim were hard at work infiltrating the Matrell estate and dismantling the conspirators. Anista, Denrick, Dregaul, and Evester would all end up dying. With Dregaul being murdered by Evester in an attempt to usurp both him and Hetta as head of the family. Grozier and his house wizard, meanwhile, managed to sneak off and escape justice for their crimes.

When all the conflict was at an end, the family sat gathered in their Marell sitting room, including Xaphira. They were left to ponder how to honor the deaths of their dishonorable family members, but in the midst of that Hetta explained why she hadn't taken control away from Dregaul sooner. She proclaimed Emriana and Vambran had exceeded her expectations in doing what was right for their family, as opposed to their House. Hetta acknowledged that the two of them were not quite ready to assume control of House Matrell quite yet, but warned them that with her old age the time to do so would be coming sooner than later.

Emriana then embraced her in a long hug, whispering that the matriarch couldn't pass away so soon, for she still had much to learn from her. With a laugh Hetta reassured her granddaughter, telling her that Xaphira was more than capable of imparting upon her any wisdom that she failed to manage. This was followed shortly thereafter by Hetta revealing to the rest of the family, much to their shock, of her secret business dealings with Xaphira over the last eleven years. And later in the discussion, when Xaphira tried to tell her niece that she still had much to learn about sneaking around, Hetta chastised that a girl at Emriana's age had no business sneaking out all the time.
Ruby Guardian
Feeling guilty over her family's hand in the death of Jithelle Skolotti, Hetta invited her mother Nimra and sister Mirolyn to come live with the Matrells. She insisted that Nimra Skolotti be made comfortable as a way of setting things right for the poor woman.

The Matrells knew that the threats against their family were far from over. Grozier and Junce Roundabout were still roaming the streets of Arrabar, they all suspected Grand Trabbar Lavant was the mastermind behind the conspirators, and they had no clue as to who the conspirators were aiming to start a war against. Feeling that the women of her household needed some time away from their troubles in Arrabar to recuperate, Hetta insisted on taking them all to their second family estate in the Chondathan countryside for a period of three tendays, whilst Kovrim and Vambran were away on campaign in far off Cimbar with the other Sapphire Crescents.

On Mirtul 10, whilst Emriana rode through the estate on her horse Honey,Chapter 2 of The Ruby Guardian, which is focused on Xaphira Matrell in Arrabar, opens with the date being Mirtul 10. And in chapter 5 it is stated that the Waukeenar holiday of Sammardach is two days away, which would imply that the proceeding chapters up to that point took place on the same day. she passed through a hedge to find Hetta out on an early morning stroll. Catching sight of her right away, Hetta waved her granddaughter over. As Emriana strode up to Hetta the matriarch commented, "Fine day for a ride, or for avoiding your lessons." Emriana snorted in response, nearly brought to laughter by her grandmother's bluntness, and tried to brush her comment off by saying the family tutors were focused on her cousins Quindy and Obiron Matrell II. Hetta quickly corrected her, saying she was referring to Emriana's lessons with Xaphir. She then informed the young woman that her aunt had just returned to their estate from Arrabar the night prior and at the stable yard getting ready to go for a ride. Elated by this news, she leaned down from Honey and gave Hetta a kiss on the cheek before trotting off to join her aunt.

When they finally arrived back at the estate and entered the dining room the pair were plainly disheveled, their riding clothes torn and soiled, with Xaphira's in particular being caked in several places with dried blood. Hetta was sat next to Ladara at the dining table, the two dining with the Skolottis and Evester's widow, Marga Talricci. They were having a meal of hard bread, peach compote, and boiled eggs in a cheese sauce. Upon seeing them, Hetta made the ironic quip, "You two look like you spent the morning stuffed in a box with a bunch of cats." As she set herself a platter, Xaphira explained how her ride with Emriana was disrupted by an attack from a group of dire jaguars, eliciting shocked gasps from the table.

Ladara was reproachful with Emriana's dangerous experience, while by contrast, Hetta was merely concerned. Sipping upon a porcelain cup of steaming Amnian tea, the Matrell matriarch remarked that she hoped their prowling around was worth it. Ladara then got herself worked up over warning her daughter to watch out for Junce Roundface, to which Hetta urged her daughter-in-law to calm herself, pointing out that Xaphira had replaced the House guards that had been foolishly loyal to Dregaul and Evester.

As an uncomfortable silence filled the room, Ladara commented her hope that Kovrim and Vambran were doing well, and her eyes began to tear up. As she dabbed at them with a napkin, Hetta gave the family her best disarming smiling and reassured her daughter-in-law that it was okay to speak of them. Further discomforting silence followed, to which Hetta sliced through by asking Embriana if she had spoken to her brother since his departure.

Hearing that she had not, Hetta sarcastically remarked that Kovrim was likely leaning over the railing of the Lady's Favor, before quickly changing her tune. She prayed Waukeen would keep the pair of them safe, as Cimbar was an unsuitable city to spend the summer in, and their summer was liable to be unpleasant if Grozier Talricci managed to start his desired war. Emriana responded that Grand Trabbar Lavant had likely sent them away so as to prevent their interference, an unsettling notion that threatened to provoke another bout of awkward silence. But before that silence could settle in Hetta clicked her tongue, telling her family not to worry themselves too much over Grozier, for the Waukeenar holy day of Sammardach was within two days and she had full intentions of the Matrells properly celebrating it when they got back to Arrabar.

The mention of Sammardach seemed to brighten the mood in the dining room, receiving a query from Emriana as to whether or not they were going to be attending the annual ball at the royal palace. To Hetta this was a certainty and the room filled with chatter of festivals and what clothing they ought to wear. In the midst of this rapt discussion, Marga slinked away, who unbeknownst to Hetta and the rest of the Matrells was being coerced by her brother Grozier to act as a spy.

The man was dead set on exacting revenge upon House Matrell for foiling his plans and taking over their business. To ensure she cooperated, he held her children hostage at the Talricci estate, replacing the real Quindy and Obiron with a pair of doppelgangers. After fleeing to her bedchambers that night Marga was greeted from the shadows by Grozier, having stepped in through a gate created by Bartimus. With hesitation Marga informed him that they were all set to return home to Arrabar the next night and that Xaphira had a lead on finding the assassin Junce.

Grozier and Bartimus came to Marga once more on Mirtul 11, who was having difficulty coping with her betrayal and the unsettling nature of the doppelgangers. He informed Marga that several members of House Matrell had gotten into unfortunate scrapes that day. Emriana and Xaphira were unlikely to return home, and the ship that had been carrying Vambran and Kovrim had sunk off the coast of Reth. This meant there were only three members of House Matrell left–Hetta and Marga's sons, Obiron and Quindy. However, being as the heirs were not of age, the responsibility of running the Matrell family business would fall to Marga.

Marga became overwrought with grief and guilt at this news, as though she herself had killed the Matrells. Marga understood what her brother was getting at, but being unable to trust her to do what he needed done, Grozier had  one of the doppelgangers shapeshift into a duplicate of Marga. Marga screamed hysterically and tried to strangle her imposter, but Bartimus cast a spell that rendered the noble woman in a state of temporal stasis.

But Emriana was far from dead, having survived her of touch with death with a group of hired thugs sent by Grozier who attempted to drown her. She awoke the next day, Mirtul 12, but was left with an appearance comparable to a common street urchin. Bedraggled, fetid, and her clothes torn in several places. Few could even recognize her as a member of a mercantile house as she made her way home.Chapter 15 of The Ruby Guardian, which takes place on the same day as chapters 12 through 14, ends with Lady Ariskrit stating that tonight was the Waukeenar holiday of Sammardach. As the holiday was established as being two days away in chapter 5, it stands to reason that the events of these later chapters must take place on Mirtul 12.

On her way home Emriana learned the distressing news that the Grand Syndar of the Temple of Waukeen had passed away and Grand Trabbar Lavant was to take up his position. Nearly one bell after she had left the temple, Emriana finally returned to the family estate, desperate to speak with Hetta. Liezl, a kitchen staffer, approached and informed her that something terrible had happened to her while she was away. It didn't take long for her to find Hetta, a large crowd of servants gathered around the body, lying in the sitting room for them to pay their respects. And waiting in the wings for her was Grozier Talricci.

As Ladara sat sobbing by Hetta's side, Grozier was all too eager to pronounce that the rest of the rightful heirs of Matrell family business had fallen, leaving it to him and Marga to take up the reigns. Emriana loudly accused him of being behind the thugs who had attacked her and Xaphira's disappearance, but she found no one willing to stand beside her against Grozier. He laid out a list of demands for Embriana to follow if she wished to remain at the estate–obey his rules every day, mind her manners, have her location known to him at all times, to cease her sneaking out from the estate, and to hand over the pendant of sending that Vambran had gifted to her on her birthday. This was the last straw for the young woman, defiant to the bitter end.

Her timorous, sobbing mother begged her to not resist, saying she wouldn't survive outside the estate. As she turned to walk away from this life, feeling hopeless, Emriana heard the faint voice of Hetta calling out to her. Ladara tried to embrace her daughter, assuming she had reconsidered, but was swiftly brushed aside as the girl knelt beside the still body of her grandmother. The voice commanded her to take Hetta's hands, which were icy to the touch. Emriana clasped them both, tears trailing down her face, and spoke silently, "Voices or no, I love you. I miss you already."

Hetta's voice then instructed her to take the ring from her right hand and as Emriana closed a hand around it she felt another presence within her. Feeling now like a voice inside her head, Hetta instructed Emriana to pocket the ring, and though stunned she did so by sliding both of the old woman's hands into her own as she bowed over her body. And at last, with urgency, Hetta instructed her to be careful not to lose it, saying that she was "inside" the ring, as well as to defy whatever demands Grozier made of her and to vacate the estate posthaste, as it was no longer safe. With a renewed sense of hope, her granddaughter did just as she was told, much to the dismay of Ladara. Rising to her feet with the pocketed ring, she told Grozier to "go to hell".

Emriana then fled to her room, Grozier in pursuit with some reluctant guards, needing to snatch up a few belongings before she made her escape. Once she was out and on the estate's lawn, she slid the ring onto her own finger, addressing her grandmother in a questioning tone, still not fully sure what was going on. Hetta reassured the girl that it was in fact her and explained the enchantment on the ring.

The matriarch knew no more than her as to what was going on, but she could tell that Grozier had done something to Marga and the twins. It was clear that Marga was acting out of character, as if some strange and unnatural force had a hold over her. She then asked the girl where Xaphira had been, only to be told that she had disappeared the night prior and wasn't answering Emriana's attempts to contact her through the pendant of sending. Whilst this news was unwelcoming, Hetta reassured her that Xaphira could handle herself and that Grozier's claims of Kovrim and Vambran both being lost as sea were not to be trusted. Hetta then instructed her to go out and seek the help of their old family friends, the Darowdryn's, at their estate.

Remembering that she could use her pendant to check up on him, Emriana stepped into an alleyway for privacy and contacted Vambran, who confirmed both he and uncle Korvim were in fact still alive. Emriana had little time to fill Vambran in on what was going on, only saying that "Something's happened to Xaphira and Hetta, and Grozier has taken over the House."

With further renewed hope, Emriana was ready to go along with Hetta's plan, but reasoned that she first had to find some way to clean herself up. There was no way she could show up to the Darowdryn estate looking and smelling as she did. As she made her way to Arrabar's harbor, she questioned Hetta once again on the ring, and received a detailed explication.

Emriana was impressed with the thorough foresight of her grandmother, but unsettled by how calm she was over the situation. She was somewhat saddend over how spirit being trapped within the ring. Hetta once more consoled her granddaughter, advising her not to overly fret on things and stay focused on their urgent matters. Wiping away the beginnings of a tear, Emriana nodded and agreed to try.

As the pair neared the inn that Emriana had in mind, they had the misfortune of running into three of the thugs that had tried to kill her the night prior–Borth, Lak, and Liathe. They were forced to flee through the streets, hoping to throw off their pursuers. With ragged breath, Emriana beseeched her grandmother on where she could go for refuge, and Hetta instructed her to head for the Darowdryn estate.

As she got closer to her destination, Emriana realized that as this section of Arrabar had few foot traffic beyond the affluent, there would be no witnesses amongst the high-walled mansions to hinder her pursuers. Worse yet, there was no sight of the City Watch. But with few other options, she took the risk. Close to the gates of the Darowdryn estate she was cut off by a female thug, Laithe, who had used arcane magic to get ahead of her. Not waiting for her pursuer to strike with a spell, Emriana sprinted over to the walls of the estate and leapt as high as she could.

Being the wall was a full story tall, Emriana wasn't able to clear the top of it, but she managed to notice and grab hold of a lone protruding stone. As she did, three magic missiles flew overhead and doubled back to strike her from behind. Afflicted with searing pain, tears welled up in her eyes and she began to lose her grip. Then to her surprise, Hetta passed on some of her energy to heal the girl and urged her onward, wearily warning, "I cannot do that for you again." Emriana mentally thanked her as she finished her ascent, looking back at the trio of thugs. As the female among the thugs began casting another spell, she rolled backwards into the Darowdryn lawn, landing in a thicket of underbrush and vines.

Pushing through the foliage into an open field, she spied the estate standing atop a central hill. In front of her, a contingent of mounted and armed guards were heading her way, whilst behind her two of the thugs landed over the wall. Though she was looking like a common thief in her bedraggled state, Hetta pressed her to head towards the guards, assured that they would recognize her name and provide protection. With no hesitation Emriana ran towards the guards, holding her arms aloft as she did so and crying out, "I yield!", until she stumbled in the grass.

The guards quickly encircled her on horseback, aiming crossbows and spears at her head. Most of them found her claims of being Emriana Matrell and needing to see Lady Ariskrit to be dubious. But then the leader of the guards strode forth, none other than the towering Tharlgarl "Steel-fists" Darowdryn, who instantly recognized her and waved the others away, confirming her story. Rising to her feet and feeling emotional over finally coming across someone she could trust, Emriana's face flooded with tears as she embraced Tharlgarl.

Left alone in the estate's parlor, Emriana was first greeted by Lady Ariskrit, whose mere appearance made the girl want to cry, being as she was still upset over Hetta's disembodied state. As the Darowdryn matriarch comforted her they were joined by Tharlgarl, who had changed from his full plate into comfortable riding attire, and shortly thereafter half a dozen other members of House Darowdryn filed into the parlor, though none of them were recognizable to the girl.

Ariskrit beckoned the girl to tell them all what had happened to hear long time friend and to not leave anything out. As she went on with her sordid tale, Emriana couldn't help but notice the rage sparking behind the matriarch's eyes. Unsure but hopeful that Hetta's spirit might overhear her, Ariskrit promised them both that her House would help them set things right. Emriana tried to thank the woman, but she pointed out that there was much planning to be done and before any of that could happen her grandson Pilos Darowdryn had his own story to share.

The young man sitting to Ariskrit's right was an Abreeant, a cleric of rank two notches above that of a novice in the Church of Waukeen, who to Emriana's relief shared her distaste for Lavant. He sat forward from his seat and told them something he had only told Ariskrit up to that point. Earlier that day, Pilos was visited by the ghost of Grand Syndar Mikolo Midelli within his bedchamber at the Temple of Waukeen. The spirit advised him to work against the warmongering machinations of Lavant and that he would find both sympathizers and the path he needed to walk back at home.

Though grateful for the support, Emriana admitted to the Darowdryns that she was uncertain what to do and that she didn't believe she had anything to offer in the plans against Grozier and Lavant. Ariskrit rebuffed her, saying she was as resourceful as Hetta to have come so far. Then remarking that they needed to know what was going on in the Matrell estate, Ariskrit turned to Tharlgarl and instructed him to go speak with Darowdryn's own House wizards, reasoning that they might have ways of uncovering what was going on and establishing a reliable channel of communication with Vambran. She felt it crucial that they know where he was and that he be informed that they had things well underway on their end.

Ariskrit then turned to Emriana and asked her to use her pendant of sending to try contacting her aunt. Xaphira luckily responded, but speaking with pain and a disoriented mind, all she could manage was to tell them she needed help from within Generon. Ariskrit reckoned this meant that Lord Eles Wianar was somehow involved and the members of House Darowdryn quickly prattled amongst themselves about the ramifications of this news. Emriana bravely proclaimed that she was going to the royal palace to rescue her, expecting some opposition, but instead Ariskrit affirmed that all of them would be going there that night, to attend the annual Sammardach ball.

The wizards of House Darowdryn would attempt several methods of locating or retrieving Xaphira. All were unfruitful, save for teaching Emriana how to use a scrying glass to spy upon a view of her belongings. They considered using teleportation magic, but wrote it off as too risky, not the least of which because of the palace's magical wards. She would need to be inside the Generon first before she could employ any magic in tracking her aunt down. Once all that planning was out of the way, Lady Ariskrit insisted that Emriana let the staff pamper her, giving her a much needed bath and rest.

Once at the celebration Emriana and Pilos stuck with the rest of the Darowdryns, up until they entered the grand ballroom, after which they drifted off and spent half a bell or more pretending to be a couple taking in the sights. Spotting guards discreetly stationed at every entryway, it seemed they had nowhere to slip out unseen. Pilos pulled her aside into a small alcolve and produced two vials from his doublet, which he explained to be potions of gaseous form. that would last them ten minutes. Examining the smokey gray liquid inside, Emriana asked Hetta what she thought. The Matrell matriarch responded, "I think you got a clever partner, and that you should hurry."

Emriana quickly asked if he had three more vials for when they find Xaphira, but unfortunately the young priest had only thought to bring two more. She assured him that they would figure something out when the time comes, then pointed out a large barred gate. She had a feeling it connected to a stable and barracks, which she reasoned would likely be connected any prison the Generon may have. Finding her logic to be sound, the two imbibed their vials.

As a pair of guards approached her in the adjacent courtyard Emriana froze and Hetta's voice spoke up, reminding her that her body is a mist and so she should stay close to the ground. And so she spread herself low, mimicking the evening mists. As the guards passed her without a second thought, Emriana could feel a sense of relief from Hetta echo in her mind. Hetta would then remain strangely silent for much of their skulking about.

The two youths drifted off through a nearby doorway, into a narrow torch-lit hall, whose first adjoining chamber was an empty kitchen that they chose to float into. Concerned of how much duration was left on her potion's effect, as she didn't want to get caught in the open, the mere thought of materializing ended the enchantment on Emriana. The young priest materialized alongside her and found her reasoning for doing so to be probably wise. He then pulled out his holy symbol, a coin on a pendant, and used it to cast a divination spell that informed him they could get to the lowest levels by following this hallway and taking a left. Unbenknownst to them, their entry was witnessed by the wizard Bartimus through a scrying mirror.

Skulking about, narrowingly avoiding being spotted by guards, the two froze up as the assassin Junce suddenly appeared at an intersection. He loudly walked off, seemingly unaware of their presence, but in reality he had planned to lure them there so he could capture Emriana. Emriana hissed at the priest to keep moving, growing agitated with his lackluster stealth. It was at that point Grandmother Hetta spoke up once more, chiding the girl that he was doing his best and reminding her that she only got so far because of his spells.

Humbled by her words, Emriana apologized to her grandmother, mentally answering back that she was just feeling scared. She then took a moment to advise him on how to make his footsteps quieter, telling him to roll his feet with each step. As they began to round the same turn Junce had taken, the prison cell from Emriana's vision came into view, standing at the base of a 'T' shaped corridor. Pressing herself to the side, Emriana slowly made her way through the strangely empty cell block, throwing knife in hand. Meanwhile, Pilos stood ready with a spell scroll in case any guards appeared.

Spotting the table of Xaphira's belongings she glimpsed from her earlier scrying, Emriana moved to examine it, unsure if it was real. Then came a muffled groan from the cell to her right and as she moved towards it her heart sank, for the voice of Junce invited her to enter. Reclined in a corner, the assassin stood over the pale and naked body of Xaphira. Acting out of instinct, Emriana’s dagger flew towards Junce’s chest, but before it could hit its mark he snatched it out of the air. Applauding her for being the most difficult of the Matrells to eliminate, he raised the dagger and used it to slice a cord in the cell, causing counterweights to raise the black cloth off of a mirror of life trapping.

Within moments of gazing upon it, Emriana seemed to vanish, as both Xaphira and her were transported into extra-dimensional cells. All she left behind were her clothes and jewelry, including the ring bound with Grandmother Hetta's spirit. For a moment they just stood in the air, outlining her missing frame, before tumbling to the ground. Pilos could do not but watch in horror as these events unfolded.

Emerald Scepter
Rather than fleeing with his potion of gaseous form, the Waukeenar priest decided to stand his ground and use his prepared spell scroll, determined to free Emriana from the mirror. He attempted to throw the dropped dagger at it, but was quickly apprehended by the timely arrival of Junce's three goons, shutting down his spell and knocking out the whining Manquillon, who was demanding to be given Xaphira as he was promised. They came to inform the assassin that his employer Lavant wanted to speak with him immediately.

The thrugs moved Pilos and Manquillon further down into the depths of the Generon, until they reached the sewers. There they began putting iron manacles around their wrists and ankles. Taking a moment to examine Emriana and Xaphira's belongings, Laithe opined that they should tie them up in a pouch around the priest's neck. That moment of interaction with the ruby ring gave Hetta the opportunity to possess the mage's body and displace her soul into the ring, giving off a momentary pained expression that Pilos recognized.

Now in control of the body, Hetta began claiming she saw something shiny like jewelry down the tunnel and goaded them on until they reluctantly acquiesced, leaving her alone with the prisoners. And for a moment, she considered leaving Maquillon behind for betraying Xaphira. Pilos thought to attack his lone captor, but before he could go through with it Hetta moved close to his ear and whispered "Don't move, let them get a little farther away first, or they will hear us." Stunned by this sudden turn, the priest wanted to demand to know what was going on, but she placed a finger to his lips.

Once Borth and Lak were far enough away, she ushered him to go back up the stairs as quietly as he could and to not look back, no matter what. Hetta and Maquillon followed from behind, torch in hand, exhorting him to keep moving as they stumbled several times in their manacles. They managed to make it beyond a gate just before the two thugs, which Hetta promptly locked shut. They frantically rattled at the bars, begging Laithe to let them free, but their pleas fell on deaf ears as Hetta set about removing the wad of cloth shoved in the two prisoners' mouths. She then looked to their manacles, but unfortunately lacked keys to unlock them with.

Hetta turned to Pilos to ask that he conjure some light for their ascent, referring to them as "those magical lights Vambran and Kovrim are so fond of," and with this it finally dawned upon the cleric what may be transpiring. Overjoyed, he asked if she was Hetta, to which she smiled and explained how she switched places with Laithe's spirit. She then quickly fetched the cleric his holy symbol so he could cast the spell, but continued to give Maquillon the cold shoulder, expressing that she had no desire to speak with him for a while. And so the three ascended, albeit a bit painfully and awkward on account of the manacles, back to the prison. However, once there they discovered that the mirror of life trapping was gone.

Luckily what the prison did have were the keys to the mancales. No longer restrained, the trio retraced the path Pilos had took previously and retreated to a library that he had hid inside in with Emriana, figuring they too should hide for a moment while they plan their next  move. But once there, Hetta sternly questioned Maquillon what had happened last evening between him, Xaphira, and Emriana.

With some resignation, the mercenary opened up about how he was overjoyed to find her still alive when she came asking for help with tracking down Junce. But the assassin found out quickly and arranged things so that he intel would lead Maquillon straight to him and five thugs. Junce gave him an ultimatum, saying he could take her away far from Arrabar if he helped him, otherwise she would be killed. Knowing what a vile man he was and not wishing to lose Xaphira again, he agreed to help. With this Hetta continued to berate the sullen man, asking if he really believed Xaphira would want anything to do with him after learning he helped the man set on destroying her family. Maquillon responded that he thought only of protecting her and thought himself, a lone man, incapable of saving her whole house. Snorting at this, Hella retorted, "You certainly can't if you don't even try. I'm glad you're not one of my children. I'm sure your mother would be very proud right now if she'd heard your little explanation."

Pilos prodded the sulking man to finish his story, inquiring what happened. Maquillon relayed that he was instructed to take her to a shed in an alley behind their arranged meeting place, a rathrur called the Silver Fish, and there Xaphira was captured in the mirror of life trapping. Junce kept promising him that he would free her once the rest of the Matrells were dealt with, but was ultimately double-crossed. Crestfallen, he asked Hetta to allow him to help rescue her, to which she accepted, saying she expected no less from him and felt that he owed Xaphira that much. Meanwhile, Pilos was pacing around nervously, remarking that he had no idea how they would go about finding the mirror. Though he had spells that could maybe ascertain its location, but their casting time was too long to guarantee anything.

Speaking like a business woman, Hetta labeled it as a "fallback idea". Maquillon then posed the idea of asking Laithe and the trio came up with a plan of having Hetta return to the gem so they could interrogate her, but gagging the body and shackling its limbs beforehand so as to ensure the mage's cooperation. Once her spirit was returned, Laithe tried to struggle, but the young priest slipped the ring off her fingers and threatened to have Hetta possess her body permanently if she didn’t tell them what they wanted to know. She acquiesced, earning the removal of her gag, but unfortunately she didn't know the mirror's whereabouts.

It wasn't long after that the door to the library slammed open and Liathe called out for Junce. Maquillon was poised to strike her, but Pilos grabbed his arm before it could. The young cleric bid the mercenary to imbibe his other potion of gaseous form, reasoning that it was time they made their escape from the Gederon. With Pilos leading the way out, the two mists drifted up to the rafters and out of the library. As they drifted through the hallway Pilos mentally asked Hetta if she could still communicate with him. She quickly confirmed as such, complimenting him on his quick thinking, and suggested that the quickest means to escape would be to ascend through the chimneys. Drifting up a fireplace, it they managed to make it onto the palace's rooftop, overlooking Arrabar.

As Pilos began contemplating having to climb the palace walls, as Emriana had suggested earlier that evening to circumnavigate the guards, he began to think wistfully of the kidnapped girl. Reading his thoughts, Hetta reassured the lad that they would find her and that Emriana could handle herself.

Meanwhile, off near the Nunwood, the druid Edilus led three members of the Order of the Sapphire Crescent–Adyan Mercatio, Horial Rohden, and Grolo–to a keyed portal, guaranteeing that it would bring them back to Arrabar. When they came through the other side they found themselves in the gardens of the Gederon and before long ran into Pilos and Maquillon. Hetta instantly recognized two of the Crescents as Vambran's childhood friends. At her urging the young priest uttered Horial's name quizzically, startling the man. He then clarified that whilst they didn't know each other, he knew Hetta Matrell, and expressed that she was glad to see him. Horial was unconvinced he could trust the stranger, until Hetta relayed through Pilos a childhood anecdote–she had spanked him, Vambran, and Adyan as youths many a time for carelessly crushing her hoplilies.

Momentarily stunned, Horial and Adyan began to chortle at the comicality of them running into each other. Wiping a tear from his eye, he asked the lad where Hetta was. A bit downcast by Hetta's situation, Adyan held out the jeweled ring. Upon Horial clutching it, Hetta asked him where Vambran was. Shocked and confused at where her voice was coming from, he stated that Vambran said she was in trouble and asked Hetta if she was back at the Matrell estate. A bit terse, Hetta clarified that she was in the ring and once more asked where Vambran was. Staring at the ring aghast under the moonlight, Horial went on to explain that Vambran sent them to help Emriana whilst he remained behind at the city-state of Reth to rescue the rest of the Sapphire Crescents.

Relief radiating from Hetta's disembodied voice at this news, she filled Horial in on their situation in brief terms, explaining that Pilos and Maquillon needed his help and that they all needed to escape the palace grounds before the guards and Junce Roundface caught them. Horial accepted these orders, bound by duty, and asked what she needed of them. Hetta told him to hand the ring back to Pilos, saying he would explain.

Right before the exchange was made, Hetta thanked Horial for coming to their aid and regarded both him and Adyan as good men, ones Vambran was lucky to call friends. Grinning at this praise, he turned to his fellow Sapphire Crescents and proclaimed Hetta said they had to escape. He then made quick introductions, turning to Pilos for orders. With the day at Godswake, at the cusp of passing into Mirtul 13, the group tried to nonchalantly stroll out the front gates. However, after no more than ten paces, they had palace guards coming at them from both sides. With little other options, they fled through various hedges and foliage, eventually losing their pursuers.

With all but Edilus feeling exhausted, Pilos jokingly remarked that he'd be tempted to surrender if the guards were still on their trail, garnering chastisement from Hetta and a feeling of displeasure radiating from the ring. Still trapped within the Gederon's walls, guards coordinating in the distance, the group discussed the possibility of climbing up a tree and began skulking around the perimeter of a pond in the garden. With the voices of guards close by, the young priest's heart raced, prompting Hetta to advise he calm down, lest he send himself "to an early grave fretting like that." Far too frightened at that moment, Pilos gave her no response. They managed to sneak their way around the palace orchards to an unguarded section of wall and through wild shaping into a lizard Edilus managed to set up a rope for them to make their escape.

The Scepters made their descend from the battlements first. Before Pilos and Maquillon could follow suit, Junce and Laithe magically appeared on the scene. Wielding a crossbow and wand respectively, they were determined to cut down their escaped prisoners. Junce closed the distance and shot Grollo with a bolt. As Edilus approached him with a scimitar, the assassin then blew on a whistle, signaling the guards. Adyan and Horial shot at Laithe from below, their missiles bouncing off of the mage, whilst Quill sacrificed his life to turn Laithe's acid spewing wand against her. And with a nasty blow to his shoulder, Edilus caused the assassin to retreat. However, the palace guards then showed up, firing crossbows upon the two remaining trespassers. With little time to set up a safe descent, Edilus shoved both him and Pilos over the edge.

As they plummeted the druid wild shaped into an eagle, clutching the priest with his talons. Though not strong enough in that form to keep Pilos aloft, the straining effort he put into beating his wings managed to slow their descent enough to make the priest's inevitable impact with the ground non-deadly. At the last moment Edilus pulled up and released his grip on Pilos, leaving him to tumble onto cobblestones, before flying off. Helped to his feet by Horial, they hurried after Adyan and the injured Grolo, the palace guards still visibly in pursuit of them. Tucking through several alleyways, they eventually reconvened with Edilus and hid inside a cellar.

Looking over the injured dwarf, Pilos used the last of his available clerical magic to cast cure minor wounds, but it accomplished little more than stopping his bleeding. Hetta then startled the priest by speaking up once more, instructing him to put the ring on Grollo, explaining that she could impart some of her energy to heal him. With that, more color returned to the dwarf's face and his breathing calmed, though he was still unconscious. The group then ditched their uniforms and had Edilus scout ahead in his eagle form. Sneaking past some guards into the nearby marketplace, they bought blankets and a merchant's whole stock of clay urns, along with the wagon and donkey carrying them. Unloading a few of the urns, they hid Grolo in the back of the wagon, hoping to pass themselves off as common laborers. Next they were convinced by Pilos to head for the Darowdryn estate, but on their way there the young priest spotted what he believed to be the face of Emriana, peeking out from behind a shop's canopied window.

Pulling their wagon up to the adjacent alley, the group found to their shock that it was indeed Emriana, alongside her aunt Xaphira. The two were tight-lipped on how they managed to escape their now gagged and bound captor, Lobra Pharaboldi. With the precarious nature of their situation, the men went along with it and stuffed Lobra into the back of the wagon alongside Grolo. Then to ensure she didn't make an escape, the two Matrell women sat atop her blanketed body.

Once back on the road, Pilos gave Emriana a satchel of her belongings. Overjoyed, the girl shouted Hetta's name and slipped on the ring, then stared off into space as she had a telepathic conversation with her. Emriana then offered the ring to Xaphira, saying that Hetta wanted to speak with her. Gingerly her aunt took and slipped it on. Whilst she became lost in her own telepathic conversation, Emriana embraced Pilos and thanked him for reuniting them. The priest then joked back at her, "It was more like her bringing me back, but you're welcome." The group then managed to reach his family estate without any further delays.

With Tharlgarl Darowdryn and twenty of his best House guards behind them, the Matrell women readied themselves to siege their own estate. Emriana decided to tell Xaphira to continue holding on to Hetta's ring, for though she missed her grandmother's presence dearly, she felt it only fair that her aunt got to spend time with her too. Riding in on horseback, Xaphira laid down the law for the guards she had hired on days prior, telling them they better show their loyalty by apprehending Grozier or never show their faces again. Intimidated by the mercenary, the guards swore their loyalty to Xaphira and servants began pouring out to welcome the two Matrells home with open arms.

But as ready for a confrontation as they were, the two were informed by several servants that Grozier and his wizard Bartimus both fled after seeing them with their armed escorts. The two then confronted Marga, who under a curse kept repeating with an emotionless expression that she didn't wish to be disturbed. However, she managed to write a note for them before they approached her, explaining and pleading for them to rescue her children from Grozier. Determined to settle things with Grozier, the Matrell women set out for the Talricci estate. They departed at Eventide, instructing Tharlgarl to stay behind and protect the rest of their family in case they failed to return.

Looking eerily abandoned, overgrown, and quiet the two Matrells made their way through the Talricci estate. Within the basement of the estate they confronted Grozier and one of his doppelgangers, with Emriana landing a killing blow on both their faces with her throwing daggers. Kicked in the shins by Quindy, Bartimus dropped the child and made a break for it with one of his portals before the Matrells could maim him as well. With nothing left to fear, the two Matrells took a moment to comfort the young twins before departing back home.

When all was said and done, the various forces poised against her family vanquished, Hetta had been apart from her body for too long to return to it. Not wanting to remain within the ring, but also recognizing that her House finally had capable successors, Hetta felt it was time for her spirit to pass on. Eventually the members of House Matrell would gather at their chapel to hold a memorial service for both her and uncle Kovrim, interring them in sarcophagi.

## Hemma

Hemma was a Dark Moon monk who was encountered by Abdel Adrian when he was young and tried to indoctrinate him. However, the Bhaalspawn followed a different path than that of a Sharan monk.

## Hellthorn

Hellthorn was an exotic variant of iron associated with the Nine Hells.

Description
It had a red hue in both its raw ore and pure metallic form.

Usage
Hellthorn took Hellish enchantments especially easily, making it an ideal medium for creating delightfully devilish tools of destruction and tyranny like staves, swords, flails, and spears. The fiendish aura of the weapon let the bearer benefit from effects that would normally only affect devils, and heightened any existing resistance to fire (such as that of a tiefling, or a ring of fire resistance). Impacts could produce blasts of flame that set the target alight, and on a failed attack it emboldened nearby devils (which included other hellthorn-wielders) to follow up immediately and attack as well. It could also be used to make a variety of iron golem fittingly called the hellthorn golem.

Availability
It was exclusively mined in the Underdark and was found near Gauntlgrym, in the form of hellthorn-infused boulders.

History
It was believed that hellthorn was not a typically occurring underground mineral, but the remnants of an ancient meteorite that struck Toril and deposited the hellthorn upon impact somewhere around Gauntlgrym. It was further speculated that its infernal powers were the result of the meteorite containing the essence of some sort of great fiendish entity, possibly even a piece of Asmodeus or a prior incarnation of him. If true, the main body of the meteorite might remain intact somewhere underground and still contain the greater whole of this fiendish power.

After taking over Gauntlgrym from the previous derro inhabitants, the duergar began extensively mining the area, recovering good amounts of iron and silver, and even a little mithral and hellthorn. This inspired leaders like Kholzourl to intensify the mining in search of ever greater hauls.

Around 1479 DR, the Ashmadai of Neverwinter sought to find a source of the metal somewhere in the region. Their leader Mordai Vell ended up dealing with the deceptive duergar merchant Nimor Ironvice, who seemed to know where to find it in the Underdark and was willing to provide it. He was actually stealing it wholesale from his fellow duergar in Gauntlgrym, and carefully smuggling it to the surface to sell at ridiculously high prices. Despite his best efforts his odd activities began to raise suspicions, and he knew he had to eventually choose between ending his side business and fleeing to surface, but was captured by his fellow duergar before he could get away with it.

During a battle to reclaim Gauntlgrym from the duergar, hellthorn boulders were hurled with catapults and hellthorn golems were a threat.

## Hekta Szind

High Priestess Hekta Szind was a powerful priestess of Lolth, who oversaw the Temple of Lolth in Sschindylryn. A prominent member of House Szind, she organized the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar and the hunt for the Thread of the Weave in the 1480s DR, all in the effort of making Lolth the new goddess of magic.

Personality

Like most servants of Lolth, Hekta was willing to do anything for her own power and the glorification of Lolth, but didn't quite understand that she was disposable in the grand scheme of things.

Abilities
Hekta called upon divine spells, including cometfall, divine punishment, enervation, fear, fire storm, hold monster, negative energy burst, and symbol of pain

Possessions
Hekta's blade of the High Priestess was a deadly implement she used in sacrificial rituals.

History

During the Darkening of Faerûn, High Priestess Hekta received a mission from her mistress Lolth—to find the Thread of the Weave and give her goddess dominion over all magic (part of the greater scheme regarding the Demon Weave). She had the Matron Mothers of Sschindylryn form an alliance together for the purpose of waging war upon the kingdom of Cormyr, all to find and capture the Thread of the Weave. With the Houses of Szind, Avithoul, and Dun'Robar taking the lead, their combined armies fell upon the village of Eveningstar, which only barely survived the first attack.

The Cormyrean counterattack on Sschindylryn targeted the weak points of the three houses, and used misdirection and sabotage to set them against each other. Once the bulk of Sschindylryn's forces were distracted, Elminster led a small team including Ana Brabener into the Sschindylryn Ziggurat with the goal of reaching the Temple of Lolth, where Hekta was performing divinations to find the Thread of the Weave. When the team reached the top of the Ziggurat, the High Priestess finished her scrying and discovered that Ana Brabener was in fact the Thread she had been searching for, and so magically compelled her to walk into a portal to the Demonweb Pits and into the arms of Lolth.

Though she had successfully completed her mission, Hekta was still forced to contend with the forces before her. She called on the guards, priestesses, and wizards of the temple, animated the temple's statues of spiders and driders, and finally summoned dretches and draegloths, but she ultimately fell in battle while crying out for Lolth's aid. In response a goristro stepped from the portal and denounced her weakness, finishing her off.
